### What G11's clean report covers

G11 reports no defects in `62d083b..0081164`, so it has nothing to reproduce. It read and ran this checkout, not that range: all its line citations (`types.cpp` 536, 971, 977, 1213, `object.hpp` 644, 670) are those of `365e55e`, which has the fixes for A11's findings, and none are those of `0081164`. So it is no evidence about `0081164`, and it does not contradict A11: its "slots.cpp correctly handle instances of built-in types and their subclasses" is false at `0081164` (RL-332) and true where it ran. It is read as a clean pass over those areas at `365e55e`. Its last probe, a `property` subclass whose `__init__` does not call `super()`, stops with a clean refusal of `property.fget`, which is not a finding. A review run in the working checkout should from now on be given a checkout of its end commit, as A11 made itself.

### Class dict views, built-in names (A11)

A11 is Codex astra-low on `62d083b..0081164`. All four findings reproduce on `dd187f7`. Probing the classes they belong to found three more, RL-332 the worst of them: A11 reports `[].__add__`, and running every special method against subclass instances showed none from `slots.cpp` bound at all. Tests `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_05.py`.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-327 | A11 #1 | A program's class named after a final built-in cannot be subclassed | Confirmed (`0081164`, mine): `class range: ...; class C(range)` raised TypeError | Fixed | `ClassObject::final_type`, set when a built-in type is made, so a name a program can also use decides nothing |
| RL-328 | A11 #2 | A view of a class's dict kept from `mro()` misses `__doc__`, `__hash__` and `__dict__` | Confirmed (`0081164`, mine). `C.__dict__` itself missed them afterwards too, since it reads the same dict | Fixed | From `mro()` on, every insertion goes through `set_attr` |
| RL-329 | A11 #3 | `[].__add__(R())` tries `R.__radd__`; so do str's and tuple's, and `__mul__`, `__rmul__` | Confirmed (RL-310, `e55e561`). `__mul__` also worded a non-index operand as the operator does | Fixed | `sequence_concat` and `sequence_repeat`: sq_concat and sq_repeat, as `wrap_binaryfunc` and `wrap_indexargfunc` call them. Every method of the built-in types against a reflecting operand now matches |
| RL-330 | A11 #4 | `_abc_init` iterates what `items()` returns twice, and each item twice | Confirmed (`0081164`, mine) | Fixed | One iterator, kept, as `PyMapping_Items` and `PySequence_Fast` do |
| RL-331 | Found on RL-327 | The same name-keyed look-up elsewhere: `class list(dict)`, `+ 1` was refused where CPython raises TypeError; a method's argument message read the table by a program's class name | Confirmed (`refuse_unimplemented_operator` from before this range) | Fixed | The row is the built-in under the class; `cpython_attribute_kind` answers nothing for a program's class |
| RL-332 | Found on RL-329 | No special method from `slots.cpp` binds through a subclass instance: `E(1).__add__(2)`, `D().__eq__(x)` raise TypeError | Confirmed (RL-310, `e55e561`); the operators work, so the corpus never showed it | Fixed | `slot()` made a plain function and set its binding afterwards, and a Builtin's class comes from its binding at construction, so it had no `__get__` |
| RL-333 | Found on RL-328 | What `mro()` sees of the class: CPython has `__dict__` there and not the slots' members; this had the reverse | Confirmed (RL-314, `e55e561`), measured on 3.15.2 | Fixed | `__dict__` goes in first; the members leave for the call and return after it, in order |

### Calls, `sys`, `re`'s buffers, what a subclass overrides (G12, A12)

G12 is gemini-3.8-flash-high on `d46c55b..1dab41b`; A12 is Codex astra-low on five of those commits, because the provider's filter stopped its run on the whole range. All nine findings reproduce on `1dab41b` against 3.15.2. A12 #4 and a suspicion from reading G12 #3 are the same defect. Each fix is in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_06.py`, where its program and what stands around it run against CPython.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-334 | G12 #1 | `f.__defaults__` longer than the parameters: `n - ndefaults` wraps, so `f()` says a required argument is missing, and too many arguments reports "from 18446744073709551614" | Confirmed: `def f(x)`, `(10, 20)`, CPython 20. The wording by reading | Fixed | `first_default` signed, as `initialize_locals`'s `m`; `too_many_positional` says what CPython says |
| RL-335 | G12 #2 | `sys.getsizeof(o, default)` ignores the default | Confirmed (`1dab41b`, mine): `__sizeof__ = None` raised TypeError, CPython 42 | Fixed | A TypeError, subclasses too, is the default; other exceptions pass, with or without one |
| RL-336 | G12 #3 | `iter(f, s)` lets `f`'s StopIteration out with what it carries | Confirmed (`a6309a4`, mine): `args (42,)`, CPython `()` | Fixed | `PyErr_Clear`, and a new one |
| RL-337 | G12 #4 | `sys.exit(T((1, 2)))` loses the tuple subclass | Confirmed (`1dab41b`, mine). Wider than reported: a plain tuple lost its identity too, `e.args is t` | Fixed | `args` is the tuple itself, `code` too with more than one item, as `_PyErr_CreateException` and `SystemExit_init` leave them. G12's account, that nothing is unpacked, is not the mechanism: one item still makes `code` that item |
| RL-338 | A12 #1 | A scanner over a bytearray does not hold its buffer, so the bytearray resizes under it | Confirmed (`a6309a4`, mine): `b.append(98)` passed, CPython BufferError | Fixed | `BytesObject::exports`, the check in every path that changes size (`PyByteArray_Resize`'s rule: the same size is no change; `take_bytes` asks outright). A scanner holds it through `_BufferHold`, released when it goes. Nothing else takes an export yet |
| RL-339 | A12 #2 | `"abc"[::Step()]` calls `__index__` twice | Confirmed (`10254b5`, mine) | Fixed | The slice is read once, and those indices decide whether the string comes back unchanged |
| RL-340 | A12 #3 | `__reduce_ex__` asks a tuple subclass for its overridden `__len__` | Confirmed (`cbc3477`, mine): ValueError, CPython accepts | Fixed | `tuple.__len__`, as `PyTuple_GET_SIZE` |
| RL-341 | A12 #4 | `iter(f, s)` misses the identity test, so a NaN or a never-equal sentinel never stops | Confirmed (`a6309a4`, mine) | Fixed | `is` first, as `PyObject_RichCompareBool`; an `__eq__` that raises leaves the iterator as it was |
| RL-342 | A12 #5 | `s.__mul__ == s.__rmul__`: two method wrappers over one function compare equal | Confirmed (`cbc3477`, mine), `(1).__add__ == (1).__radd__` was right already | Fixed | `wrapper_richcompare`: the same descriptor, by owner and name |
| RL-343 | Found on RL-338 | A `re.sub` callback could resize the bytearray being scanned | Confirmed (`a6309a4`, mine): `b'clearedcleared'`, CPython `b'BufferErrorBufferError'` with the clear refused | Fixed | `_subx` holds the export until it ends, also when the callback raises |
| RL-344 | Found on RL-340 | The same overridable look-ups elsewhere: `__getnewargs_ex__`'s dict, `stat_result`'s dict, `_sre.compile`'s `groupindex` and `indexgroup` | Confirmed on `1dab41b`, each against CPython: `__newobj__` for `__newobj_ex__`, `st_atime` 7 for 1.5, IndexError for a group that exists | Fixed | `dict.__len__`, `dict.get`, `tuple.__len__` and so on, as the C does. The same change for `__slotnames__` and the template's list is not reachable yet: subclassing `list` stops by name |
| RL-345 | Found on RL-342 | `p.match == p.prefixmatch` compared unequal: one C function under two names | Confirmed (`a6309a4`, mine): False, CPython True | Fixed | `match = prefixmatch` in `Pattern` and `SRE_Scanner` |
| RL-346 | Found on RL-335 | `__sizeof__` returning `2 ** 70` gave that number; `-(2 ** 70)` raised ValueError. CPython raises OverflowError for both | Confirmed (`1dab41b`, mine) | Fixed | `PyLong_AsSsize_t`'s check before the sign. The pre-header in the value differs from CPython (16 for 32 on a class with a dict) and is not a finding: `sys.getsizeof` values are an implementation detail (dialect spec, section 2's table) |

### `_io`'s cookies and re-init, the streams at exit, the locale guard, lazy bounds, templates, AST repr (A13, G13)

A13 is Codex astra-low and G13 gemini-3.8-flash-high, both on `1dab41b..3ea072a`. All eleven findings reproduce on `3ea072a` against 3.15.2; A13 #7 and G13 #3 are one defect. Each fix is in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_07.py`, where its program and what stands around it run against CPython.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-347 | A13 #1 | `TextIOWrapper.tell()` after a seek to a cookie with pending newline state skipped unread data: `skip_bytes` was not capped to the snapshot's length | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): `'\rc\n'`, read 1, seek(tell()), read 1, tell, read, seek back, read: CPython `'\n'`, Bella `''` | Fixed | `skip_bytes = min(b2cratio * chars_to_skip, len(next_input))`. The C asserts that invariant and, built without the assert, decodes past the snapshot into the terminating NUL when the ratio is stale after a seek; the cap gives the asserted answer, which is what the release build happened to print |
| RL-348 | A13 #2 | A replacement `sys.stdout` without a `closed` attribute made the exit flush fail: exit 120 and "Exception ignored while flushing sys.stdout" | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine) | Fixed | `file_is_closed`: no `closed`, or one that cannot be tested, means open. Found with it: CPython flushes stderr and stdout once more right after the main module (`pythonrun.c`'s `flush_io`, errors cleared), so a replacement's `flush` ran twice there and once here; `flush_io` added, in every way out of the module |
| RL-349 | A13 #3 | `TextIOWrapper.__init__` run again kept the earlier stream's decoded characters, snapshot and pending bytes | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): read 1 of `abc`, `__init__(BytesIO(b"XYZ"))`, read 2: CPython `XY`, Bella `bc`; pending writes landed in the new buffer | Fixed | `__init__` clears what the C's `Py_CLEAR`s clear |
| RL-350 | A13 #4 | Start-up coerced the C locale with `LC_ALL=C` set | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): `LC_ALL=C`: CPython `C`, Bella `C.UTF-8`; `LC_ALL=POSIX` the same | Fixed | `_Py_LegacyLocaleDetected`'s guard: a non-empty `LC_ALL` is never coerced |
| RL-351 | A13 #5 | A lazy `TypeVar` bound evaluating to `None` was evaluated at every read | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): a counting bound, CPython 1, Bella 2 | Fixed | `_ABSENT` is the C's NULL for an unevaluated bound and constraints; a computed `None` is kept |
| RL-352 | A13 #6 | `Template` iteration skipped a str subclass whose `__len__` returns 0 | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): CPython `['hello']`, Bella `[]` | Fixed | `PyUnicode_GET_LENGTH` is `str.__len__`, not the subclass's |
| RL-353 | A13 #7, G13 #3 | `AST.__repr__` detected recursion by `==`, so equal nodes were cut to `Node(...)` and a subclass's `__eq__` ran | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine) | Fixed | `Py_ReprEnter` is by identity: `any(entry is self ...)` |
| RL-354 | A13 #8 | `TextIOWrapper.tell()`'s cookie packed every field in 64 bits; the C's are `Py_off_t`, three `int`s and a `char` | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): `'\rc'`, read 1: CPython 340282367000166625996085689099021713409, Bella a 192-bit number | Fixed | `textiowrapper_build_cookie`'s layout: 8, 4, 4, 4, 1 bytes little-endian |
| RL-355 | G13 #1 | A `TypeVarTuple`'s starred default (`*Ts = *tuple[int, str]`) lost its star: `__default__` was `tuple[int, str]` | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine) | Fixed | The unpacking belongs to the lazy function, as CPython compiles it (`UNPACK_SEQUENCE 1`): the thunk unpacks one item (`[item] = value`), so eval1's text needs no star and `_typing` reads the thunk's value as is. CPython's messages for a wrong value come with it (RL-357) |
| RL-356 | G13 #2 | `Template + x` never tried `x.__radd__`: the prelude's `__add__` ran as nb_add where the C's is sq_concat | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine): CPython `custom`, Bella TypeError | Fixed | A C type with `__add__` and no `__radd__` in the names table concatenates as a sequence (`sequence_slots`), decided at presentation: Template, and deque without its hand-set flag |
| RL-357 | Found on RL-355 | Unpacking a non-iterable said "'int' object is not iterable" and too many items from a list, tuple or dict said "(expected 1)": CPython says "cannot unpack non-iterable int object" and "(expected 1, got 2)" | Confirmed (`3ea072a`, mine) | Fixed | `unpack_iterable`'s two messages in `ops.cpp`'s `unpack`, as eval1 already had them |

### `_json`'s encoder against subclasses and mutation, the code objects' names and flags (A14, G14)

A14 is Codex astra-low on `3ea072a..46a5742`; G14 is gemini-3.8-flash-high on the same range with A14's fixes in the tree, and
its two findings are on A14 #5's fix. All ten reproduce against 3.15.2. Each fix is in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_07b.py`,
where its program and what stands around it run against CPython.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-358 | A14 #1 | `_json`'s encoder classified a float by comparing the object, so a float subclass's `__ne__`, `__eq__`, `__lt__` or `__gt__` ran and could turn a finite value into `Infinity` | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): `F(1.5)` with `__ne__` returning True: CPython `1.5`, Bella `Infinity` | Fixed | `encoder_encode_float` reads `PyFloat_AS_DOUBLE`: `_encode_float` takes `float.__float__(obj)` and compares the double |
| RL-359 | A14 #2 | A `default` hook inserting a key into the dict being encoded raised RuntimeError from the dict iterator; CPython walks the dict with `PyDict_Next` and encodes the new entry | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): CPython `{"a": 1, "b": 2}`, Bella `RuntimeError` | Fixed | An exact dict is walked by the `_dict_next` primitive (the entries as they are at each step, no size check); a subclass goes through `items()` as before |
| RL-360 | A14 #3 | A list or tuple subclass was read through the base's storage; `PySequence_Fast` returns only the exact types as they are and iterates a subclass into a list | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): a tuple subclass whose `__iter__` yields 9: CPython `[9]`, Bella `[1, 2]` | Fixed | `_encode_list`'s fast sequence is the object for an exact list or tuple and `list(seq)` otherwise |
| RL-361 | A14 #4 | The note added when an item fails went through the exception's `add_note`, so an override ran and could drop the note; `_PyErr_FormatNote` calls `BaseException.add_note` itself | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): CPython `['when serializing list item 0']`, Bella printed the override's line and `[]` | Fixed | `_note` is `BaseException.add_note(exc, text)` |
| RL-362 | A14 #5 | `co_varnames` and `co_cellvars` left out an inlined comprehension's variable that a lambda captured | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): `[(lambda: i) for i in range(2)]`: CPython `('i',) ('i',)`, Bella `() ()` | Fixed | PEP 709: an inlined comprehension's cell is a fast local of the enclosing code. `resolve._inlined_varnames` lists its locals and cells; `resolve.co_cellvars` puts the parameters that are cells first, then the others sorted, the inlined comprehensions' among them; the emitter takes both from the code's meta |
| RL-363 | A14 #6 | `co_flags` lacked `CO_FUTURE_ANNOTATIONS` under `from __future__ import annotations`, and `CO_NESTED` was read off `<locals>` in the qualname, which a generic function at module level does not have | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): CPython `True True`, Bella `False False` | Fixed | The emitter sets 0x1000000 when the module's future set has `annotations`; `CO_NESTED` is whether any enclosing code is a function's (the generic's parameter scope included), tracked while collecting the codes |
| RL-364 | A14 #7 | `function.__closure__` built a new tuple at every read; CPython returns the function's stored tuple | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): `f.__closure__ is f.__closure__`: CPython True, Bella False | Fixed | The tuple is built once and kept in the function (`PyFunction::closure_tuple`), destroyed with it |
| RL-365 | A14 #8 | `scanstring`'s end index, `Scanner`'s index and `Encoder`'s indent level accepted any int; the clinic's `Py_ssize_t` converter raises OverflowError outside the C range | Confirmed (`46a5742`, mine): `_json.scanstring('"x"', 10**100)`: CPython OverflowError, Bella ValueError | Fixed | `_ssize`: `__index__`, then "Python int too large to convert to C ssize_t" outside 64 bits |
| RL-366 | G14 #1 | `co_cellvars` sorted every non-parameter cell together, so an inlined comprehension's captured variable came among the function's own cells: CPython has `('p', 'z', 'a', 'b', 'y')` where Bella had `('p', 'a', 'b', 'y', 'z')` | Confirmed (`46a5742` with A14's fixes, mine) | Fixed | CPython's `co_cellvars` is read off the locals-plus table: a cell that is a fast local too (a parameter, an inlined comprehension's variable) stands at its `co_varnames` position, the other cells follow sorted. `resolve.co_cellvars` is written that way. Found with it: a nested inlined comprehension's names come after the outer's own (`('i', 'j')`, the symbol table merges a child's after its own), where Bella put the inner's first; `_inlined_varnames` lists its own, then its comprehensions' |
| RL-367 | G14 #2 | A walrus target in an inlined comprehension was a cell of the function (`nonlocal` in the hoisted function), so it left `co_varnames` and `co_nlocals` and entered `co_cellvars`; CPython has `('i', 'x', 'r') () 3` where Bella had `('i', 'r') ('x',) 2` | Confirmed (`46a5742` with A14's fixes, mine) | Fixed | `resolve.comprehension_fast`: a cell no nested function captures (looked for through the inlined comprehensions, beside them not) is a fast local in the tables, where the function binds it; the lowering keeps its cell. A lambda in the comprehension, or a class body after it, that reads the name keeps it a cell, as CPython does |
| RL-368 | Found on RL-367 | Several walrus targets in one comprehension were bound in sorted order; CPython's `co_varnames` has them in the order the loop stores them: the conditions' and the later iterables' before the element's, a dict comprehension's key before its value | Confirmed (`46a5742` with A14's fixes, mine): CPython `('p', 'i', 'v', 'y', 'w', 's')`, Bella `('p', 'i', 'v', 'w', 'y', 's')` | Fixed | The frontend collects the targets from the hoisted loop (`_walrus_targets(inner)`), which is in that order, and `enclosing_binds` is a tuple the resolver binds in order |

### The C-module ports' argument parsing and conversions, the warnings frame, the dict views' slots (A15, G15)

A15 is Codex 6.1-sol (high) on `46a5742..c8109ba`, a partial review of the C++ runtime and the build (five findings); G15 is
gemini-argon-4 (medium) on the same range, the Python preludes and the semantics layer (nineteen). A15 #5 and G15 #16 are one
defect. Of the 23 distinct findings 21 reproduce against 3.15.2, one is a stop by design and two do not reproduce (CPython 3.15.2
prints what Bella prints). Each fix is in `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_09.py`, where its program and what stands around it
run against CPython (the 4 GiB zlib input only where the machine has the memory).

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-369 | A15 #1 | zlib's inputs were handed to the library as one `uInt`, so a buffer past 4 GiB was truncated silently: `adler32`, `crc32` and `compress` of 2^32 bytes answered for an empty one | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython `2441832825 2363233923` and 4174513 bytes, Bella `1 0` and 8 bytes | Fixed | `zlib.cpp` feeds the stream in chunks of `UINT_MAX` as zlibmodule.c's outer loop does (Z_FINISH on the last chunk only, any other flush on every chunk) and loops the checksums; a fast wrong path, which the rules forbid |
| RL-370 | A15 #2 | A dict view on the left lost to the right operand's reflected method: `{1: 0}.keys() \| Other()` with `Other.__ror__` gave 99 where the view's own `nb_or` answers first | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython `{1, 2}`, Bella `99` | Fixed | The views' algebra is now a built-in number slot in `native_binary` (`ops.cpp`), so `binary_op1` orders it as CPython orders `dictviews_as_number`; the two fallbacks after a decline are gone |
| RL-371 | A15 #3 | `items ^ items` hashed every pair before cancelling: `{'a': []}.items() ^ {'a': []}.items()` raised TypeError where CPython answers `set()` | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | `dictitems_xor` ported (`set.cpp`): a copy of the left dict, each right pair deleted when the values compare equal and hashed only when it survives, then the copy's remaining pairs |
| RL-372 | A15 #4 | `sys.excepthook(type, value, tb)` printed the value's own traceback and ignored `tb` | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython prints a traceback for a value with none, Bella not | Fixed | `_PyErr_Display`'s rule: a traceback given for a value that has none becomes the value's (`call.cpp`) |
| RL-373 | A15 #5, G15 #16 | Under `from __future__ import annotations`, a starred vararg annotation's string lost its star: `*args: *Ts` gave `'Ts'`, and the staging check declined the function's entry point | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): the dry run's MISMATCH STRING | Fixed | `pipeline/staging/annotations.py`: the strings branch keeps the star the owner recorded |
| RL-374 | G15 #1 | `warn()` asserted that a module's `__warningregistry__` is a dict; CPython passes whatever stands there to `warn_explicit`, which accepts None and raises its own TypeError for the rest | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython `None ok 1` / `TypeError 'registry' must be a dict or None`, Bella AssertionError twice | Fixed | `_setup_context` passes the value through |
| RL-375 | G15 #2 | A warning raised inside a list comprehension was charged to the line before it: the inlined helper's `frame.line` moved while the enclosing frame's, which `_frame` reads, did not | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython line 6 (the call's), Bella 4 (the statement's) | Fixed | PEP 709: an inlined comprehension's line is its enclosing frame's (`enclosing_line`, emitted by `cpp.py` for inlined code); tracebacks through a comprehension follow. Found on it: RL-392 |
| RL-376 | G15 #3 | `TokenizerIter` checked `encoding`'s type before converting `extra_tokens` (the clinic converts in parameter order after parsing), and its unexpected-keyword message had no suggestion | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | The constructor parses through `_unpack_keywords` (RL-385) and converts afterwards in order |
| RL-377 | G15 #4 | `decompressobj(zdict=...)` read the dictionary's bytes at construction; the C keeps the object and reads its buffer when the stream asks (Z_NEED_DICT), so a bytearray mutated in between is read as mutated | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython `error Error -3 while setting zdict: invalid input data`, Bella decompressed | Fixed | The object is kept and read in `run_inflate` at Z_NEED_DICT (a bytearray too), with `set_inflate_zdict`'s OverflowError past `UINT_MAX` |
| RL-378 | G15 #5 | `time`'s weekday adjustment used Python's `%`, so `tm_wday < -1` came out in range and `asctime`/`strftime` accepted it | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython `ValueError day of week out of range`, Bella `'Sat'` | Fixed | C's remainder (`_c_mod`) |
| RL-379 | G15 #6 | Seven preludes carried an `_index` of their own whose messages were CPython 3.12's (`__index__ returned non-int (type str)`) and which took `__index__ = None` for absent | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): twelve differences | Fixed | `_number_index` (the runtime's `PyNumber_Index`) is in every prelude's namespace now and the seven aliases point at it |
| RL-380 | G15 #7 | `math`, `time` and `_statistics` converted to double with their own `__float__` protocol: 3.12's message for a non-float result, and `__float__ = None` (likewise `__trunc__`, `__ceil__`, `__floor__`) taken for absent | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): eight differences | Fixed | `_as_double` (the runtime's `PyFloat_AsDouble`) in every prelude; math's special-method lookup calls what it finds, None included |
| RL-381 | G15 #8 | `_bisect` took `_contextvars.Context` for a sequence (CPython: `is not a sequence`), and named CPython's mapping types by `tp_name` alone, so a class of the program's named `dict` was refused | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | `Context` listed; `_is_c_type` (the runtime's `c_type`) guards the name test |
| RL-382 | G15 #9 | The digest types lacked DISALLOW_INSTANTIATION: `_md5.md5()` would make an uninitialised instance | Not reproduced (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython and Bella both print `TypeError cannot create '_md5.md5' instances` for all four types; the flag comes from the names table | Closed | — |
| RL-383 | G15 #10 | `_csv`, `_struct` and `time` read a str subclass through `str()` and `for c in line`, so an overridden `__str__` or `__iter__` changed a delimiter, a format or a line; the C reads the text itself | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): four differences | Fixed | `str.__str__(value)`, and iteration over it |
| RL-384 | G15 #11 | `Random.setstate` indexed the state tuple through `[]`, so a tuple subclass's `__getitem__` ran where `PyTuple_GET_ITEM` reads the array | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | `tuple.__getitem__` |
| RL-385 | G15 #12 | `_PyArg_UnpackKeywords` in the preludes: no "keyword " in "takes at most N keyword arguments" when nothing was positional; unexpected keyword reported before a missing required argument (`_hmac.new`, `math.isclose`, `_thread.start_joinable_thread`); a bool converted inside the keyword loop before later keywords were checked (the digests); no "Did you mean" in several; `_blake2`'s suggestion list without `data` | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): nine of twelve probes differed | Fixed | One primitive, `_unpack_keywords` (imports.cpp), a port of getargs.c's `_PyArg_UnpackKeywords` with `error_unexpected_keyword_arg`'s suggestion, in every prelude's namespace; zlib, _warnings, _opcode, _tokenize, _csv, _bisect, the digests, _blake2, _hmac, math.isclose and _thread parse through it. Found on it: RL-391 |
| RL-386 | G15 #13 | `_opcode.stack_effect` stops by name where CPython computes it from its tables | Reproduces; not a defect | Closed | The stop is deliberate: the stack effect is bytecode metadata (E-02), `dis.stack_effect` its only caller, and a stop by name is what the rules ask of what is absent. Reopen if a target needs it |
| RL-387 | G15 #14 | The dict views' slot wrappers all said `__or__`/`__ror__` in their messages: `{}.keys().__and__(x=1)` → "wrapper __or__() takes no keyword arguments" | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine); the sets' wrappers had the same template | Fixed | `binop_dunder(op, reflected)` names the slot (`slots.cpp`, both templates) |
| RL-388 | G15 #15 | `setup_annotations` read a dict-subclass namespace through the dict table instead of `__getitem__` | Not reproduced (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython 3.15.2 prints `[('get', '__name__')]` and `{'x': <class 'str'>}`, as Bella does; a class body's annotations go through `__annotate__` (PEP 649), not SETUP_ANNOTATIONS, so the reviewer's CPython output is another version's | Closed | — |
| RL-389 | G15 #17 | The PEP 695 lazy functions (`evaluate_bound`, `evaluate_value`, ...) named their parameter `format` in `co_varnames` and took `format=` as a positional-only passed by keyword; CPython keeps `.format` there and renames only `__annotate__`'s | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | The thunk's public name is `.format` (`frontend.py`) |
| RL-390 | G15 #18 | eval2 declared an annotate function's parameter by its public name `format`, which collides with a free or cell variable `format` of the same code (a SyntaxError or a SlotMismatch) | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | eval2 declares the hidden name and writes the public one into `co_varnames` afterwards, except where a cell or free variable has it (eval2 alone: a host code object made through `replace()` would merge the two symbols CPython keeps apart) |
| RL-391 | Found on RL-385 | A Python function's unexpected keyword had no "Did you mean" either: `f(dat=1)` for `def f(data)` | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine) | Fixed | ceval's `kw_fail` suggestion over the keyword-capable parameters (`call.cpp`) |
| RL-392 | Found on RL-375 | Lines were marked per statement, so a call on the second line of a multi-line statement was charged to the first (a traceback, a warning): CPython's instruction carries its own location | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): `[6, 9, 14]` against `[4, 8, 12]` | Fixed | L2 marks each operation's line (`lower.py`: `let` says the expression's line, operands having said theirs first) |
| RL-393 | G15 #19 | A walrus in a comprehension inside an annotation bound in the annotation scope; `symtable_extend_namedexpr_scope` passes an annotation block by and binds in the function or module around it | Confirmed (`c8109ba`, mine): CPython `module t: 42` and `outer u: 99`, Bella `MISSING` and NameError | Fixed | The annotation function is transparent in `_hoist` (`frontend.py`: the helper's `global`/`nonlocal` follow the scope around it, `binds_through` marks the helper), the resolver binds in the grandparent and the annotation scope has the name free (`resolve.py`), the well-formedness check and eval1 count the binding in the right scope (`wf.py`); the type scopes refuse such a walrus as CPython's symtable does |

### What a builtin's subclass declines and defines, the frozen modules, the record guard, staged compile()'s key (A16, G16)

A16 is Codex 6.1-sol on `2223023..98afa38` (list subclasses, exception groups, importlib, the record guard), seven findings; G16
is gemini-argon-4 (medium, through `agy`) on `2223023..726399f`, which adds staged compile(), four findings, one of them A16 #3. All
seven reproduce at `726399f` against CPython 3.15.2, compiled with `pipeline/build.py --run`, the runtime ones with exit 0 and
no stop: a wrong answer, not a refusal. Four share one cause, that the runtime asked a builtin's subclass for a method only
when it was a plain instance (`Kind::Instance`), or read its storage where CPython's slot is gone.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-394 | A16 #1 | A list subclass whose `__add__` or `__mul__` returns NotImplemented got list's concatenation or repetition of its items; CPython raises TypeError, since a class that defines `__add__` (or `__mul__`, `__rmul__`) in Python loses `sq_concat` (`sq_repeat`): `update_one_slot` finds a function where it wanted the wrapper, and those slots have no generic form | Confirmed (`726399f`): CPython `TypeError unsupported operand type(s) for +: 'L' and 'list'` (and `*`), Bella `[1, 1]` twice | Fixed | `ops.cpp`: the slots a subclass keeps (`keeps_concat`, `keeps_repeat` and the in-place two: no class of its own defines the method), gating the sequence step and `PySequence_Concat`; `builtin_concat` is the slots' work, which `list.__add__` and the others run whatever the subclass says; a subclass's number slot for + and * exists only when a class of its own defines one of the pair (`generic_number_slot`), in `operand_method` and in the reflected-first step of `binary_op1` (which had let list's own `__rmul__` wrapper answer `[1] * L()`), and `inplace` does not refuse by the operator table when a class's own method declined. 50 combinations over list, tuple and str subclasses and the other operand's `__radd__`/`__rmul__` agree with CPython (`SEQUENCE_SLOTS`); bytes subclasses stop by name, as before |
| RL-395 | A16 #2 | `callable()` answered False for an instance of a builtin's subclass that defines `__call__` (PyCallable_Check reads `tp_call`, which `slot_tp_call` fills), so `BaseExceptionGroup.subgroup` and `split` refused such a predicate | Confirmed (`726399f`): CPython `True` and `(ValueError('v'),)`, Bella `False` and TypeError | Fixed | `is_callable` asks a built-in's subclass for `__call__`, as it asks an instance (`CALLABLE`: list, int and str subclasses, `subgroup` and `split` with such a predicate) |

| RL-397 | A16 #4 | `operator.concat` and `iconcat` (PySequence_Concat, PySequence_InPlaceConcat) read a list subclass's items where its class had lost `sq_concat` and `sq_inplace_concat` to `__add__` and `__iadd__`, instead of falling back to `nb_add` and `nb_inplace_add` | Confirmed (`726399f`): CPython `add` and `iadd`, Bella `[1, 2]` twice | Fixed | With RL-394: `PySequence_Concat` and `PySequence_InPlaceConcat` take the slots the subclass keeps, else `nb_add` (and `nb_inplace_add` first for `iconcat`), else `'X' object can't be concatenated` |

| RL-399 | A16 #6 | `_frozen_importlib_external._imp` was None; CPython's module imports `_imp` (and `_io`, `_warnings`, `marshal`) at its top | Confirmed (`726399f`): CPython `True`, Bella `False` | Fixed | `bootstrap_external.py` imports `_imp`, `_io`, `sys` and `_warnings` as CPython's module does; the runtime has no `marshal`, so the module lacks it, and the two bootstrap modules now have scopes in the names table (`tools/cpython_names.py`, recorded as start-up makes them: `importlib/__init__.py` adds `_frozen_importlib.__file__`), so a name of CPython's they lack stops by name when read (`is_bootstrap_prelude` in `refuse_absent_attribute`; `STOPS`) |

### Staged texts as texts, preludes over a caller's subclass, the dry run (F17)

F17 is Claude Fable 5.2 in a session of its own on `3ea072a..d9ff83f`, asked by the author as a cross-check of rounds 14 to 16
by another model: eighteen findings out of some 40,000 differential probes. All eighteen reproduce (on `4ed0317` with the
tokenizer step in the working tree, compiled with `pipeline/build.py --run` against CPython 3.15.2). Five predate the range
(#1 to #4, #13). Three groups carry the weight: a staged text lowered as if it were a module (#1, #2, #3, #6, #8), preludes that
use Python's operators on a caller's object where the C reads storage (#4, #5, #12, #13), and the build's dry run (#7, #11, #15).
#7 is a consequence of the staging design and is the author's to decide.

Resolved in this round: thirteen fixed (RL-404 to RL-408, RL-411 to RL-413, RL-415 to RL-418, RL-420, RL-421), two that now stop by
name where they answered wrongly (RL-409, RL-414), and the two that waited for the author, decided and done on 2026-11-10: RL-410 (the
dry run is confined and the build goes on with a note) and RL-419 (the members put back, the classes guarded, the namespaces' own names
hidden and their listing stopped by name). The tests are `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_10.py`, from `STAGED_TEXTS` down.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RL-404 | F17 #1 | `eval` of a text that is one string literal returned None and stored the literal as `__doc__` in the namespace it ran in: mode "eval" wraps the expression as a module's one statement, and the frontend's docstring rule took it | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `'abc'`, Bella `None`, and the module's own `__doc__` replaced by `eval(compile("'compiled'", ...))`; eval1 and eval2 share the frontend | Fixed | `lower()` marks an expression's tree (`bella_eval`) and `_Annotations.run` takes no docstring from it (`EVAL_LITERALS`; `STAGED_TEXTS`; `THROUGH_COMPILE` for the code of a staged `compile()`) |
| RL-405 | F17 #2 | A list, set or dict comprehension at the top level of an `exec` or `eval` text read its free names from the globals alone; CPython inlines it (PEP 709), so they are LOAD_NAME: the locals given, then the globals | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `['from locals', 'from locals']` and `[2, 3]`, Bella `['from globals', ...]` and NameError | Fixed | A comprehension inlined into the module's own code is marked (`FunctionDef.module_frame`: `_hoist` sets it for the module's statements and keeps it through nested list, set and dict comprehensions, not through a generator expression, a lambda, a function or a class body), and the resolver gives its implicit globals `NameRef("name", mapping="module")`: LOAD_NAME over the module frame's mapping (`rt::load_module_name`: the locals a text was given, else the globals; `R.frame_ns` in eval2; the text's scope in eval1). A walrus in such a comprehension is declared global for the whole module, as `symtable_extend_namedexpr_scope` declares it (`L.Module.globals`). `STAGED_TEXTS`: 51 situations, on which eval1, eval2 and the compiled program agree with CPython |
| RL-406 | F17 #3 | `global` at the top level of an `exec` text was dropped (it is a no-op only where the locals are the globals): stores went to the locals | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `['counter', 'made'] 11 ['plain']`, Bella `['counter'] 10 ['counter', 'made', 'plain']` | Fixed | `L.Module.globals`: the names the module's own statements declare global (with RL-405's walrus targets); the resolver's module scope takes them, so their stores, deletes and loads are STORE_GLOBAL, DELETE_GLOBAL and LOAD_GLOBAL, and eval1's text scope has them as its globals (`STAGED_TEXTS`: every binding form after `global`) |
| RL-407 | F17 #4 | `io.StringIO.write` built its buffer with `+` and slices on the caller's object, so a str subclass's `__add__`/`__radd__` ran and the position no longer matched the text; the C copies code points | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `<p><b>bold</b></p>`, Bella `&lt;p&gt;<b>bo</p>b>`; predates the range | Fixed | `_sio_write_str` takes the text (`str.__str__`) of what is not exactly a str before it builds the buffer; the newline argument is kept as its text, an exact str (`PyUnicode_FromString`); `TextIOWrapper.write` replaces with `str.replace` (`STRINGIO`) |
| RL-408 | F17 #5 | `_heapq` read and wrote a list subclass through its `__getitem__`, `__setitem__` and `__delitem__`; `_heapqmodule.c` uses PyList_GET_ITEM, PyList_SET_ITEM and PyList_SetSlice | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython pops `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]` and logs nothing, Bella pops `[2, 2, 1, 1, 1]` and logs ten calls | Fixed | `_heapq` reads, writes and cuts through `list.__getitem__`, `list.__setitem__` and `list.__delitem__` (`HEAPQ`) |
| RL-409 | F17 #6 | `eval`/`exec` with the frame's locals, called inside a module-level comprehension, did not see the comprehension's variable (a fast local of the module's frame under PEP 709) and raised a catchable NameError where the same call in a function stops by name | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `[10, 20, 30]`, Bella `NameError("name 'r' is not defined")` | Stops by name | A frame counts the inlined comprehensions running in it (`FrameRecord::comprehensions`, from `Code::inlined`, which only a program's helpers have: the prelude stands for C), and `eval` or `exec` asked for the frame's locals there stops: `eval() in a comprehension, with the frame's locals (PEP 709: the comprehension's variables are the frame's)`. What stands around it runs (`AROUND_THE_COMPREHENSION_STOP`): the first iterable, a function called from the comprehension, a call given its namespaces. Seeing the variables comes with a frame's locals as a mapping ([P2+]) |
| RL-410 | F17 #7 | The build's dry run executes the program's module body under CPython, so a program without a main guard whose source can stage something has its writes (files, deletions, network) performed once at build time | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): from no `counter.txt`: CPython `Run(n=1)` and `1` in the file, `build.py --run` `Run(n=2)` and `2` | Fixed (the author's decision, 2026-11-10: confine the dry run and go on with a note) | The dry run's audit hook refuses what a program's module body would do to the world outside the build (a file opened for writing, removals, renames, directories, modes and times, `shutil` and `tempfile` operations, processes, a socket's connect, bind and sends); reading it and changing the process stay the body's business. The dry run of that body ends at the statement, what it staged before stays, `build.py prog.py` prints a note naming file, line and operation, and a text the body would have generated later stops by name at run time with that place, on every leg (the artefact's `unstaged["*"]`). F17-07's program now prints `Run(n=1)` and leaves `1`, twice over as CPython does; none of the corpus's 17 cases that may stage is ended early. An audit hook is no sandbox, which `docs/staging.md` says (`CONFINED_EFFECTS`, `test_the_dry_run_performs_no_effect_of_a_module_body`, `test_where_the_dry_run_is_confined`, `test_a_confined_program_runs_as_under_cpython`) |

With it: the build keeps every site of the program's own artefact, where it kept only the sites of the closure's modules; a text run with globals whose `__name__` names no module has sites of its own (`m:<module>`), and its texts stopped as not staged (found by this round's probe; `STAGED_TEXTS`, "nested exec") |
| RL-412 | F17 #9 | OrderedDict's `pop`, `move_to_end`, `copy` and iterator raised `KeyError(key)`; the C has `PyErr_SetObject(PyExc_KeyError, key)`, which spreads a tuple key into the arguments and gives None none | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `KeyError('user', 2)` and `KeyError()`, Bella `KeyError(('user', 2))` and `KeyError(None)` | Fixed | `_key_error` (`_collections.py`), measured on the pin beyond the report's cases: a tuple's items are the arguments (a subclass's too, as an exact tuple), None gives none, an instance of KeyError is the exception raised, any other object the one argument; the six `PyErr_SetObject` sites use it, `del od[k]` keeps `KeyError(key)` (`KEYERROR`) |
| RL-413 | F17 #10 | Under PEP 563 the annotation strings came from `ast.unparse`; CPython's compiler writes them with `Python/ast_unparse.c`, which differs for a call's lone generator argument, a lambda whose first parameter is starred, an f-string holding both quotes and an attribute of a bool constant | Confirmed (`4ed0317`): CPython `'max(n for n in (1, 2))'` and `'lambda*parts: parts'`, Bella `'max((n for n in (1, 2)))'` and `'lambda *parts: parts'` | Fixed | `annotation_text` (the frontend) and `_pep563_text` (the staging prototype) ask the compiler: the annotation is compiled as a parameter's under `from __future__ import annotations` and read back, so the text is `Python/ast_unparse.c`'s by construction; the frontend no longer loads `_ast_unparse` (`PEP563`; `test_annotation_text_is_the_compilers`, 25 expressions) |

Replaying the warning at each compile (category, message and line through the program's filters, a SyntaxError under `error`) is in TODO. eval1 and eval2 lower a text in the program's own process, where the host compiler's warning passes the program's filters once per text |

A str subclass's instance as a class's `__qualname__`, in `type()` or assigned, stops by name: CPython keeps the object, the runtime keeps text (`SUBCLASS_READS`, `CSV_DIALECT`, `F17_STOPS`) |

A program that ends the process leaves nothing staged, as before (`DRY_RUN_UNPARSE`, `test_a_failure_of_the_dry_run_itself_fails_the_build`) |

The names table has the two modules' classes' own attributes (`_frozen_importlib.FrozenImporter:find_spec`; `_SEP` left out, which the class gets at its first file name), and one a class lacks stops by name (`refuse_in`). What the runtime keeps in the two namespaces for itself (89 and 10 names) is no attribute of the modules: a read does not find it, as in CPython, a deletion raises CPython's AttributeError, an assignment stops by name, and `dir()`, `vars()` and `__dict__` of the two modules stop by name. F17-16's program prints CPython's three lines. Found by comparing the two preludes with the pin's text and fixed with it: seven docstrings of `SourceLoader`'s and `NamespacePath`'s had gone, `cache_from_source` was an older release's (a `debug_override` parameter, no `fspath` of the path), `set_data`'s `_mode` default was 0, the methods under `@_check_name` and the `@_requires_*` ones had parameters of their own; a test now holds every shared function to the pin's parameters and docstring. And a bound method's `__doc__` was the method type's docstring, not its function's (`method_get_doc`), for any class of any program (`BOOTSTRAP_CLASSES`, `BOOTSTRAP_DOCS`, `BOOTSTRAP_STOPS`, `METHOD_DOC`, `test_the_bootstrap_preludes_keep_the_pins_parameters_and_docstrings`) |

### The tokenizer, the F17 fixes, `atexit` (R18): nothing found

R18 (Codex astra-low, 2026-11-10) read `d9ff83f..28f1105` in a checkout of its own at `28f1105` and reports no finding. Its evidence is three programs
run under CPython and compiled: 17 tokenizer inputs whose token tuples, positions and exception arguments agree; `exec` with globals
and locals, comprehension names, a walrus, a signature of every kind of parameter, a handler of `atexit` before a global's finaliser
and `sys.modules["threading"] = None` at the end; the heap, csv and json preludes over a caller's subclasses, and `atexit` handlers
that register and unregister while they run. It says itself that this is targeted evidence, not coverage, and that it ran no full
test suite. Nothing to reproduce and no ID assigned. What this session found after that commit is under RL-419 and in DECISIONS of
2026-11-10 (a bound method's `__doc__`, the bootstrap preludes' drift from the pin's text); both lie in code older than the range.

### What a module body leaves for later, and the hook's own arguments (R19)

R19 (gemini-3.8-flash, 2026-11-10) read `28f1105..17f9262` in a checkout of its own at `17f9262`. Both findings are in the confinement
of the dry run, written the same day for RL-410. Its other probes (the importer classes' members, a bound method's `__doc__`, the two
hooks, `os.scandir`, `os.walk`, `glob`, `shutil.rmtree`) agreed with CPython; by its own account targeted checks, with no full test run.
The tests are `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_10.py`, from `DEFERRED_EFFECTS` down.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RL-422 | R19 #1 | The confinement held only while a module body ran: what a body left for later ran unconfined in the dry run's process, so an `atexit` callback that writes a file wrote it during a build, with no note | Confirmed (`fbe23e2`): `build.py effect.py` alone, with no `--run`, leaves `effect` in the file and prints no note; CPython's one run appends one line | Fixed | From the moment a module of the program's begins to run, every effect event in the dry run's process is refused, whoever performs it and whenever (a body, a thread it started, a finaliser, a callback), but for the tool's own writing of its artefact and `tempfile`'s probe; and the process ends with `os._exit` once the artefact is written, so what a body left for the end of the process (handlers of `atexit` and of `threading`, finalisers, threads still running) does not run at build time at all. A refusal is recorded, for the note and the run-time reason, when it ends a module body (the main thread, a body running). Measured before the rule was widened: none of the closure's 183 modules raises such an event while it is imported, so nothing of the standard library's is refused. The reviewer's program leaves no file after a build and one line per run (`DEFERRED_EFFECTS`, `test_what_a_module_body_leaves_for_later_is_confined_too`) |
| RL-423 | R19 #2 | The hook formatted every `socket.*` event's second argument; `socket.sethostname` has one, so the hook itself raised IndexError: the body ended without a record, the build printed no note and the program's refusal had no reason | Confirmed (`fbe23e2`): `sys.audit("socket.sethostname", "foo")` between two `exec` calls: no note at build, `E-01: exec at __main__:<module> of a text the dry run did not stage` with no place at run time; CPython exits 0 | Fixed | The hook reads an event's arguments as they come: a socket's address where there are two, the one argument otherwise, none at all, and a repr that raises; an `open` is judged by its flags only when it has them. The record, the note and the reason are made each time. Left as it is and said here: a program's own `sys.audit("os.remove", ...)` is taken for the operation, since the hook cannot tell the two apart, so the reviewer's program builds with the note and stops by name with the reason where CPython, in which the event does nothing, exits 0 (`HOOK_ARGUMENTS`, `test_the_confinement_takes_whatever_arguments_an_event_has`) |

### The window of the tool's own writing, what raises no event, a query taken for an effect (R20)

R20 (gemini-3.8-flash, 2026-11-10; the one report of the round that reached this log) read `17f9262..0dcf6b8` in a checkout of its own at `0dcf6b8`. All five findings are again in the
confinement of the dry run, in the version RL-422 gave it. The tests are `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_10.py`, from
`WINDOW` down.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RL-424 | R20 #1 | While the tool wrote its artefact a counter let every effect through, in any thread: a thread the program started, retrying its write, got it through that window | Confirmed (`0dcf6b8`): `build.py window.py` alone leaves the thread's file on the host | Fixed | The tool's own writing is recognised by what it is, not by when: an `open` or a rename of one of the artefact's own files, performed by `main` itself on the main thread (`_own_write`). The counter is gone, so no window is. The reviewer's thread keeps trying through three dry runs and never writes (`WINDOW`, `test_the_tools_own_writing_lets_nothing_else_through`) |
| RL-425 | R20 #2 | `os.mkfifo` and `os.mknod` raise no audit event, so the hook never saw them: a module body made a named pipe on the host during a build | Confirmed (`0dcf6b8`): `build.py fifo.py` alone leaves the pipe; `Modules/posixmodule.c` has no `PySys_Audit` in either | Fixed | What raises no audit event cannot be refused by a hook, so it is replaced beforehand: when the program's code starts, `mkfifo`, `mknod`, `setpriority`, `sched_setaffinity`, `sched_setparam`, `sched_setscheduler` and `tcsetpgrp` in `posix` and `os`, and `pidfd_send_signal` in `_signal`, become refusals that go through the same record and note. The list comes from reading every function of `Modules/posixmodule.c` for `PySys_Audit`: 148 of this platform's raise none, and these are the ones that reach beyond the process without a descriptor opened for writing first, which is refused; the rest read, or act on the process itself. With it the dry run's standard input is the null device, so a body that reads descriptor 0 does not take the build's (`CONFINED_EFFECTS`, `CONFINED_ALLOWED`) |
| RL-426 | R20 #3 | `os.lockf(fd, os.F_TEST, n)`, which asks whether a lock is held and changes nothing, was refused as an effect: the body ended there with a note | Confirmed (`0dcf6b8`): the note names `os.lockf(3)` at the query's line | Fixed | `os.lockf` is an effect unless its command is `F_TEST`; `fcntl.flock` and `fcntl.lockf`, added with it, unless they give a lock up (`_is_effect`; `CONFINED_ALLOWED`, `CONFINED_EFFECTS`). The reviewer's program builds with no note; compiled it stops at `os.lockf`, which the runtime does not have yet |
| RL-427 | R20 #4 | `shutil.chown`, `webbrowser.open` and `signal.pthread_kill` raise events the list did not have | Confirmed in part (`0dcf6b8`): the three pass the hook. The first two announce an effect on the host that the calls beneath them would be refused for anyway (`os.chown`, a process), so naming them refuses sooner and says it better. `signal.pthread_kill` signals a thread of the dry run's own process: nothing outside it changes, and a body that ends the process leaves nothing staged, as before: not a defect | Fixed in part | `shutil.chown` and `webbrowser.open` are in the list, and `syslog.syslog` with them (it writes to the system's log). `signal.pthread_kill` is not: see the verdict (`CONFINED_EFFECTS`, `CONFINED_ALLOWED`) |
| RL-428 | R20 #5 | The dry run ends with `os._exit` after flushing `sys.__stdout__` and `sys.__stderr__`; output held by a wrapped `sys.stdout` or `sys.stderr` would be lost | Not reproduced, and the report gives no program: the dry run is a process of its own, `main` puts the interpreter's two streams back before it returns, on every way out, and those are the two that are flushed; the tool's own failure writes its traceback to that `sys.stderr`, which is line-buffered. Left as it is | Not a defect | |
| RL-429 | R20, the other reviewer's probe `review-r20/xattr_order.py` | `os.getxattr` and `os.removexattr` converted `follow_symlinks` before `attribute`: an attribute whose `__fspath__` and a flag whose `__bool__` are observable ran in the wrong order, and where the flag's conversion changes what the attribute gives, another attribute was read and removed | Confirmed (`bb3f1e2`): CPython prints `attribute user.first`, `follow`, reads `b'first'` and leaves `user.second`; compiled it printed `follow`, `attribute user.second`, read `b'second'` and removed it. The other reviewer of the round left no report in the worktree (the second's took its name), but this probe and its two recorded outputs were there, and differ. Argument Clinic converts in the order of the parameters (`path`, `attribute`, `follow_symlinks`); the prelude's `_xattr_path` took the flag with the path. The order of every other function of `os` that converts more than one argument was measured with it (37 calls: `stat`, `lstat`, `mkdir`, `access`, `readlink`, `symlink`, `link`, `open`, `chmod`, `fchmod`, `copy_file_range`, `sendfile`, `utime`, `unlink`, `remove`, `rmdir`, and an object that is an index and a path at once): the same as CPython's | Fixed | `follow_symlinks` is converted in its turn, last, in the four functions (`_xattr_follow` in `runtime/prelude/posix.py`), and the check that a descriptor is not given with it comes after, as in the C. The reviewer's program agrees; `XATTR_ORDER` (`test_the_extended_attributes_convert_their_arguments_in_order`) compares seventeen calls on one machine, since a file system may lack the attributes; and the order of the other functions is case C-7-61, which passed before the change and keeps it so |

(3234866). It could not build (a check chain was running), so every finding came with CPython's evidence and Bella's code path,

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### What a class's hooks may see, what a handler may break, where a frame is (S22, A22)

The author asked for a review of the range since F21, `3234866..bc3f700`, by a swarm of Sonnet reviewers and by Codex's Astra at low
effort, on 2026-11-19. Both worked in one worktree at `bc3f700` with a build wrapper that let two builds run at a time; each reviewer
wrote a report of its own. Every finding was run again by the session on both sides before a verdict: the thirteen of A22 one by one,
the 61 programs of S22 in one batch (each differs from CPython as its report says), five by reading. Seven findings are in both
reviews. 79 findings are 68 rows: 62 confirmed defects, three closed as the specification's (D-20, E-04 twice), three for the
author. Most are older than the range and were found through it; RL-457, RL-458 and RL-460 are of the range's own commits.
The round's commit fixes 34 of the 62 and one more found on the way (RL-522): the two crashes, the hang, the report of an
exception, the generators, what the frontend and the emitter got wrong, and the smaller ones that were quick. The other 28
are open, each of low severity (an object made to misbehave, a message's words), and listed in TODO.

Before the worktree was removed, all 140 of the reviewers' probe programs were run again, under CPython and the fixed build: 74 agree on
standard output, masked standard error and exit status. Of the other 66, 14 end in a stop by name, and every differing row of the
rest was matched: to an open row above, to a deviation (D-20's codecs and pure-Python `datetime` and `decimal`, D-25's places, D-27,
D-28, addresses), to RL-222, to what varies between runs (process ids, the clock, an unseeded `random`, set order without a fixed
hash seed), or to one of three things no report named: RL-523, RL-524 and RL-525. The rest of RL-461 (a source line that is a str
subclass kept its new line) was found the same way and fixed. One suspicion did not hold: a coding line with an empty spec before
a valid one differs only because its encoding, cp1252, is outside the phase-1 codecs (D-20); with latin-1 the two sides agree.

The tests are `pipeline/tests/test_review_2026_11_19.py`.

| ID | Source | Finding | Verdict | Status | Evidence and resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RL-454 | A22-01, S22 generators #2 | While `throw()` makes the exception of a class, the generator was still suspended, so the class's `__init__` or `__new__` could close or advance it | Confirmed: `g.throw(E, None)` with an `E.__init__` that calls `g.close()` gave E in the caller; CPython marks the frame executing first, the close fails with ValueError and that is what is thrown in. Older than the range (one argument too) | Fixed | `pend_exception` (`runtime/src/generator.cpp`) marks the generator executing while the exception is made and puts the state back either way |
| RL-455 | A22-02, S22 generators #5 | An error from looking up `throw` on the object a generator delegates to was thrown into the delegating generator | Confirmed: a `throw` property that raises LookupError: CPython raises it in the caller and the generator stays suspended (`next` gives the child's value); compiled the generator's `except` caught it. Older than the range | Fixed | The lookup of `throw` is outside the `try` whose error goes into the generator; its own error leaves the generator suspended |
| RL-456 | A22-03, S22 generators #6 | While a delegate's `throw` runs, the delegating generator was not on the running stack | Confirmed: `traceback.extract_stack()` in the delegate's `throw` lists `<module>, parent, throw` in CPython, `<module>, throw` compiled; and the DeprecationWarning of an inner generator's `throw` reached through it names the `yield from` line in CPython, the caller's compiled (`_gen_throw` links the frame for the call) | Fixed | `LinkedFrame`: the generator's code, module and line are the running frame for the length of the delegate's call |
| RL-457 | A22-04, A22-11, S22 numbers #1, replaced #6 | `struct`'s float and complex codes and `time`'s clock id let a BaseException from `__float__`, `__complex__` or `__index__` through | Confirmed: KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit, a BaseException of the program's: CPython answers every failed conversion there with `struct.error` or TypeError; the handlers moved for RL-448 still said `except Exception` | Fixed | The three handlers say `except BaseException` |
| RL-458 | A22-05 | `complex(Real(), Imag())` released the temporary `__complex__` gave after converting the imaginary part | Confirmed: a `__del__` on the temporary prints before `Imag.__float__` in CPython (`complex_new_impl` drops it once its two doubles are read), after it compiled. A class whose call goes through `type`'s own call keeps it (`MyError(*t).args is t`, `StopIteration(*t).args is t`, a bound `e.__init__(*t)`, `partial(MyError)(*t).args is t`, `itemgetter(*t)` with more than one item, an untyped `lru_cache` without keywords: no `__hash__` call for a tuple that has its hash); a built-in exception class that inherits both `__new__` and `__init__` from BaseException is called through a vectorcall of its own and makes a new tuple (`Exception(*t).args is t` and `KeyError(*t).args is t` are False), as do `OSError`, an unbound `BaseException.__new__(cls, *t)`, a call with two stars, and every function written in Python. | RL-698 | The class behind A31's findings (2026-11-21) | A prelude reaches a method that a subclass of a built-in type overrides, where the C reads the object's own data | Counted by the reviewer's instrumented subclasses of str, int, float, bytes, bytearray, tuple, list and dict, every overridable method of which logs its name: 32 of the 41 findings are this one cause in 16 preludes, and its sweep is not the whole of it. Where a C function has tested its argument's type it reads the data through the concrete API (`PyUnicode_*`, `PyBytes_AS_STRING`, `PyLong_As*`, `PyTuple_GET_ITEM`, `PyList_GET_ITEM`, `PyDict_Next`), which calls nothing of the class's; the prelude wrote `len(x)`, `x[i]`, `x == y`, `i in x`, `for c in x`, `bytes(x)`, `int(x)` on the object. Four of the results are wrong data: a digest of other bytes, `compare_digest` true for unequal bytes, a JSON text with another key and value, a struct field with another byte | Fixed | Two primitives every prelude has: `_own_data(x)` (for an instance of a subclass of str, bytes, int, float or tuple the exact base object with the same data; anything else as it is) and `_buffer_bytes(x)` (the octets of a bytes or a bytearray as an exact bytes); the mutable ones are read by their base type's methods where the C reads them live (`list.__len__` and `list.__getitem__` at each turn, `dict.__contains__`, `bytearray.__setitem__`). 17 preludes changed (`_io`, `_struct`, `zlib`, `binascii`, the five hashes, `_hmac`, `_operator`, `_locale`, `_sre`, `posix`, `_codecs`, `_warnings`, `_json`, `_functools`, `itertools`, `_typing`), and a UnicodeError keeps its start and end as plain ints. The sweep is the reviewer's driver, kept as `pipeline/tests/test_subclass_data.py` with `subclass_data_sweep.txt`: 462 calls in two modes, a process each; 244 of the 924 runs differed before, none now but the 21 rows of functions that stop by name and one that is D-20 (`hashlib.new` without OpenSSL looks the name up in a dict, as a CPython built without it does). Not the whole of it: the sweep covers the functions the reviewer tried |

| RL-883 | A39-09 | A recursion through `lru_cache` or `partial` 20 000 deep succeeds where CPython raises RecursionError | Confirmed as an observation, and no defect: it is the decision of Question 28 seen from the other side. Each level of such a recursion goes through C in CPython and uses its native stack, 8 MiB by a shell's default, which ends near 20 000 levels; the compiled program has 256 MiB. Where either side stops past the depths a program may count on is the machine's and the policy's. D-17's words say CPython reaches the bound "later or not at all"; here it reaches it earlier, and the words should allow that: put to the author. 10 rows (`stack/{cached,part}-*` under the default and the soft limit, `config-1024/cached-*`) | As decided | Done 2026-11-24 (Question 38: B; spec v0.34): D-17's and section 8's words say CPython reaches the bound at another depth, or not at all. Was: No change: Question 28's decision, seen from CPython's side. D-17's words ("later or not at all") are put to the author to allow "earlier" |
| RL-884 | A39-10 | On a small stack a compiled program raises RecursionError at depths CPython completes | Confirmed as an observation, and as decided: D-17's bound. Under a hard limit (`ulimit -s 1024`, `-s 65536`) or a policy of 1, 2 or 16 MiB the compiled program has that much stack, and 20 000 plain calls or data nested 3 000 deep do not fit; CPython's Python frames are not on the stack. Before Question 28's step this was so under every limit. The yield-from chains of `stackedge` end CPython itself by a segmentation fault under 1 MiB, where the compiled program raises RecursionError or finishes. 60 rows (`stack/limit-*`, `stackedge/*`, `config-{1,2,16}/*`) | As decided | No change: D-17's bound, and the rule that the process's hard limit is kept, which the author was told with the step |

The policy's reader takes any whole number from 1 up, and the runtime turns MiB into bytes by a shift that leaves nothing of 2**44 in 64 bits, so the program stays on the process's own stack: `RecursionError, 0` for `0, 0` under a soft limit of 1 MiB and a hard one of 64 MiB, where a policy of 1024 gives 64 MiB. 6 rows (`config-wrap/*`, `config-huge/*`) | Fixed | The policy's reader takes 1 to 2**20 MiB (1 TiB) and refuses anything else when the program is built; the runtime's conversion to bytes cannot wrap. The two suites are refused at build now. A test holds the range |
